Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well… mostly it’s my eating, I guess. It’s gotten all turned around lately, and it’s starting to worry me. Some days I just completely forget to eat lunch, like the whole middle of the day disappears and then later I realize, “Oh, I never had anything.” And then other days I’m just standing there at the kitchen counter eating crackers or sweets, not even really enjoying them, just sort of picking and picking.

It’s strange because nothing tastes quite right, but I keep doing it anyway. It doesn’t feel like normal hunger exactly, and it doesn’t feel like a proper meal either. It’s just… off. That’s what made me think I should come in and talk to someone.

I’m not really sure about actual pounds, to be honest. I haven’t been keeping close track on the scale. Not really in the sense of, you know, looking in the mirror and worrying I’m getting heavy. That’s not what’s been on my mind. It’s more that my eating just feels all wrong lately, and that worries me.

And then other days I’m at the counter eating crackers or sweets, a little here and a little there, even though they don’t taste right and I don’t even especially want them. So I’m not sitting there thinking, “Oh no, I’ll gain weight,” exactly. I’m more bothered that I don’t seem to have a normal appetite or normal meals right now.

No, I don’t think I’d call it a fear of gaining weight. That’s not really the thing that’s upsetting me.

What made me come in is more that my eating has gotten all turned around. Like I was saying, some days I just forget lunch completely, not because I’m trying not to eat, but because it doesn’t even seem to come into my mind. And then other days I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, just little bits, and it’s not even satisfying because nothing tastes quite right. I’m not thinking, “Oh, I shouldn’t eat because I’ll gain weight.” It’s more like I don’t understand why my appetite is acting this way.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It bothers me, yes, though I guess I’d say it’s not the worst thing in the world… but it’s enough that I notice it and it upsets me. What made me come in is that my eating feels all turned around. I’ll have days where I forget lunch completely, and then later I think, “Why didn’t I eat?” And other days I’m just standing there at the counter eating crackers or sweets, but nothing tastes right, so it doesn’t even feel normal or satisfying.

So the distress is more that it feels unlike me, if that makes sense. It makes me feel unsettled, like I can’t get a regular rhythm with meals, and I don’t quite understand why. Not panicked exactly, but concerned enough that it weighs on me.

I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting very differently, no—not in any big way that I could point to. I’m not, like, suddenly needing different pants or anything like that.

It’s more the eating itself that feels off to me. What made me come in is that my meals have gotten sort of disorganized. Some days I’ll just forget lunch completely, and then later I realize I never ate, and other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets here and there, even though they don’t really taste right. So I can’t say I’ve noticed a big change in how my clothes fit, but I have noticed that my appetite and eating pattern don’t feel normal for me.

What I’ve noticed more is that my eating is all mixed up—some days I forget lunch completely, like it just doesn’t occur to me until much later, and then other days I’m sort of standing at the counter picking at crackers or sweets. It’s not even that they taste good exactly… nothing tastes quite right, but I keep nibbling anyway. So I don’t know if I’ve lost weight, but my appetite and eating pattern definitely feel off.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really in the sense of, you know, looking in the mirror and worrying I’m getting heavy. That’s not what’s been on my mind. It’s more that my eating just feels all wrong lately, and that worries me.\n\nSome days I’ll realize I’ve skipped lunch entirely, not on purpose, just… it’s like the day slips by and I never think to make anything. And then other days I’m at the counter eating crackers or sweets, a little here and a little there, even though they don’t taste right and I don’t even especially want them. So I’m not sitting there thinking, “Oh no, I’ll gain weight,” exactly. I’m more bothered that I don’t seem to have a normal appetite or normal meals right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you fear gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’d call it a fear of gaining weight. That’s not really the thing that’s upsetting me.\n\nWhat made me come in is more that my eating has gotten all turned around. Like I was saying, some days I just forget lunch completely, not because I’m trying not to eat, but because it doesn’t even seem to come into my mind. And then other days I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, just little bits, and it’s not even satisfying because nothing tastes quite right. I’m not thinking, “Oh, I shouldn’t eat because I’ll gain weight.” It’s more like I don’t understand why my appetite is acting this way.\n\nDoctor: How much distress do these changes in your eating pattern cause you?\n\nPatient: It bothers me, yes, though I guess I’d say it’s not the worst thing in the world… but it’s enough that I notice it and it upsets me. What made me come in is that my eating feels all turned around. I’ll have days where I forget lunch completely, and then later I think, “Why didn’t I eat?” And other days I’m just standing there at the counter eating crackers or sweets, but nothing tastes right, so it doesn’t even feel normal or satisfying.\n\nSo the distress is more that it feels unlike me, if that makes sense. It makes me feel unsettled, like I can’t get a regular rhythm with meals, and I don’t quite understand why. Not panicked exactly, but concerned enough that it weighs on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in the way your clothes fit you lately?\n\nPatient: I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting very differently, no—not in any big way that I could point to. I don’t think it’s exactly emotions that trigger it, no… at least not in a clear way, like “I’m sad so I eat,” or “I’m upset so I don’t eat.” It doesn’t feel that direct to me.

What I’ve noticed is more that my eating has gotten all turned around. Some days I’ll just forget lunch completely, and it’s not like I’m sitting there making a decision about it—I’ll just realize later, “Oh, I never ate.” And then other days, I’ll find myself standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, just picking at things, but it doesn’t even feel satisfying because nothing tastes quite right. So I don’t know if that’s emotions exactly. It feels more like my appetite and my routine are just off, and I can’t quite get them back into a normal pattern.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, I think so… not completely, like I never want food, but it’s definitely not the same as it used to be. Food just doesn’t pull me in the way it did before. What really made me come in is that my eating has gotten all turned around.

Some days I’ll realize I forgot lunch entirely, and that’s not like me. I won’t even feel hungry in the normal way, or I just don’t think about it until later. Then other days it’s the opposite sort of thing—I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, just picking at them, but even then nothing tastes right. It’s not enjoyable exactly. It’s more like I’m eating because it’s there, but I don’t get much satisfaction from it.

So yes, I’d say my interest in food is less, or at least it feels off and kind of flattened. It’s not a normal appetite for me.

Yes… I think I do enjoy it less now. It’s not that food disgusts me or anything like that, but it just doesn’t have the same pull, and a lot of the time it doesn’t taste right.

It feels more like my appetite and my routine are just off, and I can’t quite get them back into a normal pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less interest in food than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so… not completely, like I never want food, but it’s definitely not the same as it used to be. Food just doesn’t pull me in the way it did before. What really made me come in is that my eating has gotten all turned around.\n\nSome days I’ll realize I forgot lunch entirely, and that’s not like me. I won’t even feel hungry in the normal way, or I just don’t think about it until later. Then other days it’s the opposite sort of thing—I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, just picking at them, but even then nothing tastes right. It’s not enjoyable exactly. It’s more like I’m eating because it’s there, but I don’t get much satisfaction from it.\n\nSo yes, I’d say my interest in food is less, or at least it feels off and kind of flattened. It’s not a normal appetite for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you enjoy food less often now than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I do enjoy it less now. It’s not that food disgusts me or anything like that, but it just doesn’t have the same pull, and a lot of the time it doesn’t taste right.\n\nThat’s really what made me come in, because my eating has gotten all turned around. Some days I’ll completely forget lunch, like the whole middle of the day goes by and then later I realize I never ate. Yes… yes, I think there is less pleasure in it than there used to be. I don’t want to overstate it like every single bite is awful, but it’s not the same. What really made me come in is that my eating has gotten all turned around.

Some days I’ll go right past lunch and not even notice until later, like it just slips out of the day completely. And then other days I’ll be standing there at the counter, eating crackers or something sweet, and I’m not even really enjoying it. It’s just there, so I pick at it. But nothing tastes quite right, even things I used to like. So the pleasure part is definitely less… or maybe it’s more that eating feels strange and unsatisfying now, instead of normal.

And then other days I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, just little bits, and it’s not even satisfying because nothing tastes quite right. I’m not thinking, “Oh, I shouldn’t eat because I’ll gain weight.” It’s more like I don’t understand why my appetite is acting this way.\n\nDoctor: How much distress do these changes in your eating pattern cause you?\n\nPatient: It bothers me, yes, though I guess I’d say it’s not the worst thing in the world… but it’s enough that I notice it and it upsets me. What made me come in is that my eating feels all turned around. I’ll have days where I forget lunch completely, and then later I think, “Why didn’t I eat?” And other days I’m just standing there at the counter eating crackers or sweets, but nothing tastes right, so it doesn’t even feel normal or satisfying.\n\nSo the distress is more that it feels unlike me, if that makes sense. It makes me feel unsettled, like I can’t get a regular rhythm with meals, and I don’t quite understand why. Not panicked exactly, but concerned enough that it weighs on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in the way your clothes fit you lately?\n\nPatient: I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting very differently, no—not in any big way that I could point to. I’m not, like, suddenly needing different pants or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more the eating itself that feels off to me. What made me come in is that my meals have gotten sort of disorganized. Some days I’ll just forget lunch completely, and then later I realize I never ate, and other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets here and there, even though they don’t really taste right. So I can’t say I’ve noticed a big change in how my clothes fit, but I have noticed that my appetite and eating pattern don’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your eating is triggered by your emotions?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s exactly emotions that trigger it, no… at least not in a clear way, like “I’m sad so I eat,” or “I’m upset so I don’t eat.” It doesn’t feel that direct to me.\n\nWhat I’ve noticed is more that my eating has gotten all turned around. Some days I’ll just forget lunch completely, and it’s not like I’m sitting there making a decision about it—I’ll just realize later, “Oh, I never ate.” And then other days, I’ll find myself standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, just picking at things, but it doesn’t even feel satisfying because nothing tastes quite right. So I don’t know if that’s emotions exactly. It feels more like my appetite and my routine are just off, and I can’t quite get them back into a normal pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less interest in food than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so… not completely, like I never want food, but it’s definitely not the same as it used to be. Food just doesn’t pull me in the way it did before. What really made me come in is that my eating has gotten all turned around.\n\nSome days I’ll realize I forgot lunch entirely, and that’s not like me. I won’t even feel hungry in the normal way, or I just don’t think about it until later. Then other days it’s the opposite sort of thing—I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, just picking at them, but even then nothing tastes right. It’s not enjoyable exactly. It’s more like I’m eating because it’s there, but I don’t get much satisfaction from it.\n\nSo yes, I’d say my interest in food is less, or at least it feels off and kind of flattened. It’s not a normal appetite for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you enjoy food less often now than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I do enjoy it less now. It’s not that food disgusts me or anything like that, but it just doesn’t have the same pull, and a lot of the time it doesn’t taste right.\n\nThat’s really what made me come in, because my eating has gotten all turned around. Some days I’ll completely forget lunch, like the whole middle of the day goes by and then later I realize I never ate. And then other days I’ll be standing at the counter picking at crackers or sweets, but not really enjoying them—just sort of eating because they’re there. Even things that should taste good don’t really taste right. So yes, I’d say I enjoy food less often than I used to, or at least it feels much less normal and satisfying.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have less pleasure when eating than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think there is less pleasure in it than there used to be. I don’t want to overstate it like every single bite is awful, but it’s not the same. What really made me come in is that my eating has gotten all turned around.\n\nSome days I’ll go right past lunch and not even notice until later, like it just slips out of the day completely. And then other days I’ll be standing there at the counter, eating crackers or something sweet, and I’m not even really enjoying it. It’s just there, so I pick at it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d call it stress or anxiety exactly. I mean, I’m sure when I’m wound up or worried it doesn’t help, but this feels a little more… I don’t know, like my eating itself has gotten all mixed up.

That’s really what made me come in. Some days I’ll just completely forget lunch, and I don’t mean I’m trying not to eat or anything. It’s more like the day goes by and later I realize, oh, I never had anything. And then other days I do the opposite—I’ll stand at the counter and eat crackers or sweets, just picking at them, but not because I’m enjoying it. Nothing tastes quite right, so it doesn’t feel satisfying.

So maybe stress is part of it sometimes, but it doesn’t feel only like nerves. It feels like my appetite and interest in food are just turned around from how they used to be.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not really sure about “significant” weight loss. I haven’t been keeping close track of the number, to be honest. What I noticed more is that my eating has just gotten all turned around, and that’s really what made me come in.

Some days I’ll forget lunch completely—not on purpose, just the day goes by and I realize later I never ate. Then other days I do the opposite and I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, sort of picking at them, even though I’m not enjoying it and nothing tastes right. So whether my weight has changed a lot, I don’t know for sure, but my appetite and the way I’m eating definitely feel different.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if there’s one other thing I can point to, like one clear reason. It’s more that the whole pattern has changed, and that’s what bothers me.

I’ve said it a bit already, but some days lunch just disappears from my mind completely. I’ll look up later and realize I never ate, not because I was trying to skip it, just because it didn’t occur to me. And then on other days, it’s almost the opposite — I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers, or something sweet, just sort of nibbling and picking at it. But I’m not really enjoying it. It’s not like, “Oh, this tastes good.” It’s just… there.

And even foods I used to like don’t taste quite right to me now. So I don’t know if something else is making it less enjoyable, exactly. It feels more like my appetite and the pleasure in eating have gotten all turned around.

I haven’t been keeping close track on the scale. What I’ve noticed more is that my eating is all mixed up—some days I forget lunch completely, like it just doesn’t occur to me until much later, and then other days I’m sort of standing at the counter picking at crackers or sweets. It’s not even that they taste good exactly… nothing tastes quite right, but I keep nibbling anyway. So I don’t know if I’ve lost weight, but my appetite and eating pattern definitely feel off.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really in the sense of, you know, looking in the mirror and worrying I’m getting heavy. That’s not what’s been on my mind. I’m more bothered that I don’t seem to have a normal appetite or normal meals right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you fear gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’d call it a fear of gaining weight. That’s not really the thing that’s upsetting me.\n\nWhat made me come in is more that my eating has gotten all turned around. Like I was saying, some days I just forget lunch completely, not because I’m trying not to eat, but because it doesn’t even seem to come into my mind. And then other days I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, just little bits, and it’s not even satisfying because nothing tastes quite right. I’m not thinking, “Oh, I shouldn’t eat because I’ll gain weight.” It’s more like I don’t understand why my appetite is acting this way.\n\nDoctor: How much distress do these changes in your eating pattern cause you?\n\nPatient: It bothers me, yes, though I guess I’d say it’s not the worst thing in the world… but it’s enough that I notice it and it upsets me. What made me come in is that my eating feels all turned around. I’ll have days where I forget lunch completely, and then later I think, “Why didn’t I eat?” And other days I’m just standing there at the counter eating crackers or sweets, but nothing tastes right, so it doesn’t even feel normal or satisfying.\n\nSo the distress is more that it feels unlike me, if that makes sense. It makes me feel unsettled, like I can’t get a regular rhythm with meals, and I don’t quite understand why. Not panicked exactly, but concerned enough that it weighs on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in the way your clothes fit you lately?\n\nPatient: I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting very differently, no—not in any big way that I could point to. I’m not, like, suddenly needing different pants or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more the eating itself that feels off to me. What made me come in is that my meals have gotten sort of disorganized. Some days I’ll just forget lunch completely, and then later I realize I never ate, and other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets here and there, even though they don’t really taste right. So I can’t say I’ve noticed a big change in how my clothes fit, but I have noticed that my appetite and eating pattern don’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your eating is triggered by your emotions?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s exactly emotions that trigger it, no… at least not in a clear way, like “I’m sad so I eat,” or “I’m upset so I don’t eat.” It doesn’t feel that direct to me.\n\nWhat I’ve noticed is more that my eating has gotten all turned around. Some days I’ll just forget lunch completely, and it’s not like I’m sitting there making a decision about it—I’ll just realize later, “Oh, I never ate.” And then other days, I’ll find myself standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, just picking at things, but it doesn’t even feel satisfying because nothing tastes quite right. So I don’t know if that’s emotions exactly. It feels more like my appetite and my routine are just off, and I can’t quite get them back into a normal pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less interest in food than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so… not completely, like I never want food, but it’s definitely not the same as it used to be. Food just doesn’t pull me in the way it did before. What really made me come in is that my eating has gotten all turned around.\n\nSome days I’ll realize I forgot lunch entirely, and that’s not like me. I won’t even feel hungry in the normal way, or I just don’t think about it until later. Then other days it’s the opposite sort of thing—I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, just picking at them, but even then nothing tastes right. It’s not enjoyable exactly. It’s more like I’m eating because it’s there, but I don’t get much satisfaction from it.\n\nSo yes, I’d say my interest in food is less, or at least it feels off and kind of flattened. It’s not a normal appetite for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you enjoy food less often now than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I do enjoy it less now. It’s not that food disgusts me or anything like that, but it just doesn’t have the same pull, and a lot of the time it doesn’t taste right.\n\nThat’s really what made me come in, because my eating has gotten all turned around. Some days I’ll completely forget lunch, like the whole middle of the day goes by and then later I realize I never ate. And then other days I’ll be standing at the counter picking at crackers or sweets, but not really enjoying them—just sort of eating because they’re there. Even things that should taste good don’t really taste right. So yes, I’d say I enjoy food less often than I used to, or at least it feels much less normal and satisfying.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have less pleasure when eating than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think there is less pleasure in it than there used to be. I don’t want to overstate it like every single bite is awful, but it’s not the same. What really made me come in is that my eating has gotten all turned around.\n\nSome days I’ll go right past lunch and not even notice until later, like it just slips out of the day completely. It feels more like my appetite and my routine are just off, and I can’t quite get them back into a normal pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less interest in food than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so… not completely, like I never want food, but it’s definitely not the same as it used to be. Food just doesn’t pull me in the way it did before. What really made me come in is that my eating has gotten all turned around.\n\nSome days I’ll realize I forgot lunch entirely, and that’s not like me. I won’t even feel hungry in the normal way, or I just don’t think about it until later. Then other days it’s the opposite sort of thing—I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, just picking at them, but even then nothing tastes right. It’s not enjoyable exactly. It’s more like I’m eating because it’s there, but I don’t get much satisfaction from it.\n\nSo yes, I’d say my interest in food is less, or at least it feels off and kind of flattened. It’s not a normal appetite for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you enjoy food less often now than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I do enjoy it less now. It’s not that food disgusts me or anything like that, but it just doesn’t have the same pull, and a lot of the time it doesn’t taste right.\n\nThat’s really what made me come in, because my eating has gotten all turned around. Some days I’ll completely forget lunch, like the whole middle of the day goes by and then later I realize I never ate. And then other days I’ll be standing at the counter picking at crackers or sweets, but not really enjoying them—just sort of eating because they’re there. Even things that should taste good don’t really taste right. So yes, I’d say I enjoy food less often than I used to, or at least it feels much less normal and satisfying.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have less pleasure when eating than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think there is less pleasure in it than there used to be. I don’t want to overstate it like every single bite is awful, but it’s not the same. What really made me come in is that my eating has gotten all turned around.\n\nSome days I’ll go right past lunch and not even notice until later, like it just slips out of the day completely. And then other days I’ll be standing there at the counter, eating crackers or something sweet, and I’m not even really enjoying it. It’s just there, so I pick at it. But nothing tastes quite right, even things I used to like. So the pleasure part is definitely less… or maybe it’s more that eating feels strange and unsatisfying now, instead of normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in food is related to any stress or anxiety you might be feeling?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it stress or anxiety exactly. I mean, I’m sure when I’m wound up or worried it doesn’t help, but this feels a little more… I don’t know, like my eating itself has gotten all mixed up.\n\nThat’s really what made me come in. Some days I’ll just completely forget lunch, and I don’t mean I’m trying not to eat or anything. It’s more like the day goes by and later I realize, oh, I never had anything. And then other days I do the opposite—I’ll stand at the counter and eat crackers or sweets, just picking at them, but not because I’m enjoying it. Nothing tastes quite right, so it doesn’t feel satisfying.\n\nSo maybe stress is part of it sometimes, but it doesn’t feel only like nerves. It feels like my appetite and interest in food are just turned around from how they used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about “significant” weight loss. I haven’t been keeping close track of the number, to be honest. What I noticed more is that my eating has just gotten all turned around, and that’s really what made me come in.\n\nSome days I’ll forget lunch completely—not on purpose, just the day goes by and I realize later I never ate. Then other days I do the opposite and I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, sort of picking at them, even though I’m not enjoying it and nothing tastes right. Yes… yes, I think so. It’s not just the food. I used to look forward to things, you know? I had my watercolor class, and my quilting circle, and book club. Those were the things that gave shape to the week.

Now I’ll get the bag ready sometimes — like the watercolor things or whatever I’m supposed to bring — and I’ll leave it by the door, and then when it’s time to go I just… don’t. I look at it sitting there and I can’t seem to make myself care enough to pick it up and leave.

It’s not that I hate those people or that I decided I don’t like the activities anymore. I used to enjoy them. I just don’t feel that pull toward them like I used to, and then I feel bad afterward because I know I’ve missed something I normally would’ve cared about.

And then other days I’ll be standing there at the counter, eating crackers or something sweet, and I’m not even really enjoying it. It’s just there, so I pick at it. So the pleasure part is definitely less… or maybe it’s more that eating feels strange and unsatisfying now, instead of normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in food is related to any stress or anxiety you might be feeling?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it stress or anxiety exactly. I just don’t feel that pull toward them like I used to, and then I feel bad afterward because I know I’ve missed something I normally would’ve cared about.\n\n[Last Question]\nWhen did you start noticing you weren't enjoying your activities like watercolor class, quilting circle, and book club as much as you used to?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 74 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Depressive Episode Duration\nmanifestation: \"The low, colorless stretch has been with me for about a year and two months.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s been… a long while now. I’d say I first really noticed it a little over a year ago — maybe about a year and two months, if I’m trying to put a time on it.

At first I thought it was just a bad week, or that I was tired of the same routine. You know, sometimes you don’t feel like going out, and I told myself that was all it was. But then weeks kept passing, and the things that used to feel colorful, like watercolor class especially — which is funny, because it’s literally color — started feeling kind of flat. Like the color had drained out of it.

So yes, about fourteen months or so. It’s been hanging around that long, this low, colorless stretch.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… mostly it’s my eating, I guess. It’s gotten all turned around lately, and it’s starting to worry me. Some days I just completely forget to eat lunch, like the whole middle of the day disappears and then later I realize, “Oh, I never had anything.” And then other days I’m just standing there at the kitchen counter eating crackers or sweets, not even really enjoying them, just sort of picking and picking.\n\nIt’s strange because nothing tastes quite right, but I keep doing it anyway. It doesn’t feel like normal hunger exactly, and it doesn’t feel like a proper meal either. It’s just… off. That’s what made me think I should come in and talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about actual pounds, to be honest. I haven’t been keeping close track on the scale. What I’ve noticed more is that my eating is all mixed up—some days I forget lunch completely, like it just doesn’t occur to me until much later, and then other days I’m sort of standing at the counter picking at crackers or sweets. It’s not even that they taste good exactly… nothing tastes quite right, but I keep nibbling anyway. So I don’t know if I’ve lost weight, but my appetite and eating pattern definitely feel off.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really in the sense of, you know, looking in the mirror and worrying I’m getting heavy. That’s not what’s been on my mind. It’s more that my eating just feels all wrong lately, and that worries me.\n\nSome days I’ll realize I’ve skipped lunch entirely, not on purpose, just… it’s like the day slips by and I never think to make anything. And then other days I’m at the counter eating crackers or sweets, a little here and a little there, even though they don’t taste right and I don’t even especially want them. So I’m not sitting there thinking, “Oh no, I’ll gain weight,” exactly. I’m more bothered that I don’t seem to have a normal appetite or normal meals right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you fear gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’d call it a fear of gaining weight. That’s not really the thing that’s upsetting me.\n\nWhat made me come in is more that my eating has gotten all turned around. Like I was saying, some days I just forget lunch completely, not because I’m trying not to eat, but because it doesn’t even seem to come into my mind. And then other days I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, just little bits, and it’s not even satisfying because nothing tastes quite right. I’m not thinking, “Oh, I shouldn’t eat because I’ll gain weight.” It’s more like I don’t understand why my appetite is acting this way.\n\nDoctor: How much distress do these changes in your eating pattern cause you?\n\nPatient: It bothers me, yes, though I guess I’d say it’s not the worst thing in the world… but it’s enough that I notice it and it upsets me. What made me come in is that my eating feels all turned around. I’ll have days where I forget lunch completely, and then later I think, “Why didn’t I eat?” And other days I’m just standing there at the counter eating crackers or sweets, but nothing tastes right, so it doesn’t even feel normal or satisfying.\n\nSo the distress is more that it feels unlike me, if that makes sense. It makes me feel unsettled, like I can’t get a regular rhythm with meals, and I don’t quite understand why. Not panicked exactly, but concerned enough that it weighs on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in the way your clothes fit you lately?\n\nPatient: I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting very differently, no—not in any big way that I could point to. I’m not, like, suddenly needing different pants or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more the eating itself that feels off to me. What made me come in is that my meals have gotten sort of disorganized. Some days I’ll just forget lunch completely, and then later I realize I never ate, and other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets here and there, even though they don’t really taste right. So I can’t say I’ve noticed a big change in how my clothes fit, but I have noticed that my appetite and eating pattern don’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your eating is triggered by your emotions?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s exactly emotions that trigger it, no… at least not in a clear way, like “I’m sad so I eat,” or “I’m upset so I don’t eat.” It doesn’t feel that direct to me.\n\nWhat I’ve noticed is more that my eating has gotten all turned around. Some days I’ll just forget lunch completely, and it’s not like I’m sitting there making a decision about it—I’ll just realize later, “Oh, I never ate.” And then other days, I’ll find myself standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, just picking at things, but it doesn’t even feel satisfying because nothing tastes quite right. So I don’t know if that’s emotions exactly. It feels more like my appetite and my routine are just off, and I can’t quite get them back into a normal pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less interest in food than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so… not completely, like I never want food, but it’s definitely not the same as it used to be. Food just doesn’t pull me in the way it did before. What really made me come in is that my eating has gotten all turned around.\n\nSome days I’ll realize I forgot lunch entirely, and that’s not like me. I won’t even feel hungry in the normal way, or I just don’t think about it until later. Then other days it’s the opposite sort of thing—I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, just picking at them, but even then nothing tastes right. It’s not enjoyable exactly. It’s more like I’m eating because it’s there, but I don’t get much satisfaction from it.\n\nSo yes, I’d say my interest in food is less, or at least it feels off and kind of flattened. It’s not a normal appetite for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you enjoy food less often now than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I do enjoy it less now. It’s not that food disgusts me or anything like that, but it just doesn’t have the same pull, and a lot of the time it doesn’t taste right.\n\nThat’s really what made me come in, because my eating has gotten all turned around. Some days I’ll completely forget lunch, like the whole middle of the day goes by and then later I realize I never ate. And then other days I’ll be standing at the counter picking at crackers or sweets, but not really enjoying them—just sort of eating because they’re there. Even things that should taste good don’t really taste right. So yes, I’d say I enjoy food less often than I used to, or at least it feels much less normal and satisfying.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have less pleasure when eating than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think there is less pleasure in it than there used to be. I don’t want to overstate it like every single bite is awful, but it’s not the same. What really made me come in is that my eating has gotten all turned around.\n\nSome days I’ll go right past lunch and not even notice until later, like it just slips out of the day completely. And then other days I’ll be standing there at the counter, eating crackers or something sweet, and I’m not even really enjoying it. It’s just there, so I pick at it. But nothing tastes quite right, even things I used to like. So the pleasure part is definitely less… or maybe it’s more that eating feels strange and unsatisfying now, instead of normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in food is related to any stress or anxiety you might be feeling?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it stress or anxiety exactly. I mean, I’m sure when I’m wound up or worried it doesn’t help, but this feels a little more… I don’t know, like my eating itself has gotten all mixed up.\n\nThat’s really what made me come in. Some days I’ll just completely forget lunch, and I don’t mean I’m trying not to eat or anything. It’s more like the day goes by and later I realize, oh, I never had anything. And then other days I do the opposite—I’ll stand at the counter and eat crackers or sweets, just picking at them, but not because I’m enjoying it. Nothing tastes quite right, so it doesn’t feel satisfying.\n\nSo maybe stress is part of it sometimes, but it doesn’t feel only like nerves. It feels like my appetite and interest in food are just turned around from how they used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about “significant” weight loss. I haven’t been keeping close track of the number, to be honest. What I noticed more is that my eating has just gotten all turned around, and that’s really what made me come in.\n\nSome days I’ll forget lunch completely—not on purpose, just the day goes by and I realize later I never ate. Then other days I do the opposite and I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, sort of picking at them, even though I’m not enjoying it and nothing tastes right. So whether my weight has changed a lot, I don’t know for sure, but my appetite and the way I’m eating definitely feel different.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything else going on that's making food less enjoyable for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there’s one other thing I can point to, like one clear reason. It’s more that the whole pattern has changed, and that’s what bothers me.\n\nI’ve said it a bit already, but some days lunch just disappears from my mind completely. I’ll look up later and realize I never ate, not because I was trying to skip it, just because it didn’t occur to me. And then on other days, it’s almost the opposite — I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers, or something sweet, just sort of nibbling and picking at it. But I’m not really enjoying it. It’s not like, “Oh, this tastes good.” It’s just… there.\n\nAnd even foods I used to like don’t taste quite right to me now. So I don’t know if something else is making it less enjoyable, exactly. It feels more like my appetite and the pleasure in eating have gotten all turned around.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you enjoy activities other than eating less often now than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think so. Those were the things that gave shape to the week.\n\nNow I’ll get the bag ready sometimes — like the watercolor things or whatever I’m supposed to bring — and I’ll leave it by the door, and then when it’s time to go I just… don’t. I look at it sitting there and I can’t seem to make myself care enough to pick it up and leave.\n\nIt’s not that I hate those people or that I decided I don’t like the activities anymore. I used to enjoy them. I just don’t feel that pull toward them like I used to, and then I feel bad afterward because I know I’ve missed something I normally would’ve cared about.\n\nDoctor: When did you start noticing you weren't enjoying your activities like watercolor class, quilting circle, and book club as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a long while now. I’d say I first really noticed it a little over a year ago — maybe about a year and two months, if I’m trying to put a time on it.\n\nAt first I thought it was just a bad week, or that I was tired of the same routine. You know, sometimes you don’t feel like going out, and I told myself that was all it was. But then weeks kept passing, and the things that used to feel colorful, like watercolor class especially — which is funny, because it’s literally color — started feeling kind of flat. Like the color had drained out of it.\n\nSo yes, about fourteen months or so. It’s been hanging around that long, this low, colorless stretch.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Every now and then I think I do, or at least I think I should. Like I’ll tell myself, “You used to like watercolor, just go, you’ll feel better once you’re there.” And sometimes I even get the bag ready — the paints, the little pad, whatever I’m supposed to bring — and I’ll set it by the door so I can’t miss it.

But then when it’s time to leave, I just stand there looking at it. It’s not exactly that I decide not to go. It’s more like there’s no pull in me. The part that used to look forward to it just doesn’t seem to show up. Same with quilting circle, or book club. I used to have that feeling of, “Oh good, it’s Thursday, I get to go,” and now I mostly just don’t feel enough interest to get myself out the door.

So yes, I have days where I think about doing them, but actually wanting to go — really feeling that spark again — that doesn’t happen much.

I used to have that feeling of, “Oh good, it’s Thursday, I get to go,” and now I mostly just don’t feel enough interest to get myself out the door.\n\nSo yes, I have days where I think about doing them, but actually wanting to go — really feeling that spark again — that doesn’t happen much.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever feel like you've gotten your interest back in those activities, even if it's just for a little while?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 74 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Loss_of_Interest\nmanifestation: \"I used to look forward to watercolor class, quilting circle, and book club, but now I often leave the bag by the door and just can’t make myself care enough to go.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Maybe for a flicker, but not really in the way I used to. Sometimes I’ll get this little idea in my head like, “Oh, maybe I’ll go to watercolor today,” and I’ll gather the paints and the paper and put the bag right by the door, because I’m trying to help myself along, you know? Like if it’s sitting there, then I’ll have no excuse.

But then the time comes and I just look at it. I know I used to look forward to that class, and to quilting circle, and book club too. I used to have that nice feeling of having somewhere to be, something I cared about. Now even when I try to push myself toward it, I can’t seem to make myself care enough to actually pick up the bag and go.

So I guess I might remember that I used to enjoy it, but feeling the interest come back… only briefly, if at all. It doesn’t last long enough to get me out the door most of the time.

    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.8, F50.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… mostly it’s my eating, I guess. It’s gotten all turned around lately, and it’s starting to worry me. Some days I just completely forget to eat lunch, like the whole middle of the day disappears and then later I realize, “Oh, I never had anything.” And then other days I’m just standing there at the kitchen counter eating crackers or sweets, not even really enjoying them, just sort of picking and picking.\n\nIt’s strange because nothing tastes quite right, but I keep doing it anyway. It doesn’t feel like normal hunger exactly, and it doesn’t feel like a proper meal either. It’s just… off. That’s what made me think I should come in and talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about actual pounds, to be honest. I haven’t been keeping close track on the scale. What I’ve noticed more is that my eating is all mixed up—some days I forget lunch completely, like it just doesn’t occur to me until much later, and then other days I’m sort of standing at the counter picking at crackers or sweets. It’s not even that they taste good exactly… nothing tastes quite right, but I keep nibbling anyway. So I don’t know if I’ve lost weight, but my appetite and eating pattern definitely feel off.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really in the sense of, you know, looking in the mirror and worrying I’m getting heavy. That’s not what’s been on my mind. It’s more that my eating just feels all wrong lately, and that worries me.\n\nSome days I’ll realize I’ve skipped lunch entirely, not on purpose, just… it’s like the day slips by and I never think to make anything. And then other days I’m at the counter eating crackers or sweets, a little here and a little there, even though they don’t taste right and I don’t even especially want them. So I’m not sitting there thinking, “Oh no, I’ll gain weight,” exactly. I’m more bothered that I don’t seem to have a normal appetite or normal meals right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you fear gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’d call it a fear of gaining weight. That’s not really the thing that’s upsetting me.\n\nWhat made me come in is more that my eating has gotten all turned around. Like I was saying, some days I just forget lunch completely, not because I’m trying not to eat, but because it doesn’t even seem to come into my mind. And then other days I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, just little bits, and it’s not even satisfying because nothing tastes quite right. I’m not thinking, “Oh, I shouldn’t eat because I’ll gain weight.” It’s more like I don’t understand why my appetite is acting this way.\n\nDoctor: How much distress do these changes in your eating pattern cause you?\n\nPatient: It bothers me, yes, though I guess I’d say it’s not the worst thing in the world… but it’s enough that I notice it and it upsets me. What made me come in is that my eating feels all turned around. I’ll have days where I forget lunch completely, and then later I think, “Why didn’t I eat?” And other days I’m just standing there at the counter eating crackers or sweets, but nothing tastes right, so it doesn’t even feel normal or satisfying.\n\nSo the distress is more that it feels unlike me, if that makes sense. It makes me feel unsettled, like I can’t get a regular rhythm with meals, and I don’t quite understand why. Not panicked exactly, but concerned enough that it weighs on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in the way your clothes fit you lately?\n\nPatient: I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting very differently, no—not in any big way that I could point to. I’m not, like, suddenly needing different pants or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more the eating itself that feels off to me. What made me come in is that my meals have gotten sort of disorganized. Some days I’ll just forget lunch completely, and then later I realize I never ate, and other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets here and there, even though they don’t really taste right. So I can’t say I’ve noticed a big change in how my clothes fit, but I have noticed that my appetite and eating pattern don’t feel normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your eating is triggered by your emotions?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s exactly emotions that trigger it, no… at least not in a clear way, like “I’m sad so I eat,” or “I’m upset so I don’t eat.” It doesn’t feel that direct to me.\n\nWhat I’ve noticed is more that my eating has gotten all turned around. Some days I’ll just forget lunch completely, and it’s not like I’m sitting there making a decision about it—I’ll just realize later, “Oh, I never ate.” And then other days, I’ll find myself standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, just picking at things, but it doesn’t even feel satisfying because nothing tastes quite right. So I don’t know if that’s emotions exactly. It feels more like my appetite and my routine are just off, and I can’t quite get them back into a normal pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have less interest in food than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so… not completely, like I never want food, but it’s definitely not the same as it used to be. Food just doesn’t pull me in the way it did before. What really made me come in is that my eating has gotten all turned around.\n\nSome days I’ll realize I forgot lunch entirely, and that’s not like me. I won’t even feel hungry in the normal way, or I just don’t think about it until later. Then other days it’s the opposite sort of thing—I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, just picking at them, but even then nothing tastes right. It’s not enjoyable exactly. It’s more like I’m eating because it’s there, but I don’t get much satisfaction from it.\n\nSo yes, I’d say my interest in food is less, or at least it feels off and kind of flattened. It’s not a normal appetite for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you enjoy food less often now than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I do enjoy it less now. It’s not that food disgusts me or anything like that, but it just doesn’t have the same pull, and a lot of the time it doesn’t taste right.\n\nThat’s really what made me come in, because my eating has gotten all turned around. Some days I’ll completely forget lunch, like the whole middle of the day goes by and then later I realize I never ate. And then other days I’ll be standing at the counter picking at crackers or sweets, but not really enjoying them—just sort of eating because they’re there. Even things that should taste good don’t really taste right. So yes, I’d say I enjoy food less often than I used to, or at least it feels much less normal and satisfying.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have less pleasure when eating than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think there is less pleasure in it than there used to be. I don’t want to overstate it like every single bite is awful, but it’s not the same. What really made me come in is that my eating has gotten all turned around.\n\nSome days I’ll go right past lunch and not even notice until later, like it just slips out of the day completely. And then other days I’ll be standing there at the counter, eating crackers or something sweet, and I’m not even really enjoying it. It’s just there, so I pick at it. But nothing tastes quite right, even things I used to like. So the pleasure part is definitely less… or maybe it’s more that eating feels strange and unsatisfying now, instead of normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of interest in food is related to any stress or anxiety you might be feeling?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it stress or anxiety exactly. I mean, I’m sure when I’m wound up or worried it doesn’t help, but this feels a little more… I don’t know, like my eating itself has gotten all mixed up.\n\nThat’s really what made me come in. Some days I’ll just completely forget lunch, and I don’t mean I’m trying not to eat or anything. It’s more like the day goes by and later I realize, oh, I never had anything. And then other days I do the opposite—I’ll stand at the counter and eat crackers or sweets, just picking at them, but not because I’m enjoying it. Nothing tastes quite right, so it doesn’t feel satisfying.\n\nSo maybe stress is part of it sometimes, but it doesn’t feel only like nerves. It feels like my appetite and interest in food are just turned around from how they used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about “significant” weight loss. I haven’t been keeping close track of the number, to be honest. What I noticed more is that my eating has just gotten all turned around, and that’s really what made me come in.\n\nSome days I’ll forget lunch completely—not on purpose, just the day goes by and I realize later I never ate. Then other days I do the opposite and I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, sort of picking at them, even though I’m not enjoying it and nothing tastes right. So whether my weight has changed a lot, I don’t know for sure, but my appetite and the way I’m eating definitely feel different.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything else going on that's making food less enjoyable for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there’s one other thing I can point to, like one clear reason. It’s more that the whole pattern has changed, and that’s what bothers me.\n\nI’ve said it a bit already, but some days lunch just disappears from my mind completely. I’ll look up later and realize I never ate, not because I was trying to skip it, just because it didn’t occur to me. And then on other days, it’s almost the opposite — I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers, or something sweet, just sort of nibbling and picking at it. But I’m not really enjoying it. It’s not like, “Oh, this tastes good.” It’s just… there.\n\nAnd even foods I used to like don’t taste quite right to me now. So I don’t know if something else is making it less enjoyable, exactly. It feels more like my appetite and the pleasure in eating have gotten all turned around.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you enjoy activities other than eating less often now than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think so. It’s not just the food. I used to look forward to things, you know? I had my watercolor class, and my quilting circle, and book club. Those were the things that gave shape to the week.\n\nNow I’ll get the bag ready sometimes — like the watercolor things or whatever I’m supposed to bring — and I’ll leave it by the door, and then when it’s time to go I just… don’t. I look at it sitting there and I can’t seem to make myself care enough to pick it up and leave.\n\nIt’s not that I hate those people or that I decided I don’t like the activities anymore. I used to enjoy them. I just don’t feel that pull toward them like I used to, and then I feel bad afterward because I know I’ve missed something I normally would’ve cared about.\n\nDoctor: When did you start noticing you weren't enjoying your activities like watercolor class, quilting circle, and book club as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a long while now. I’d say I first really noticed it a little over a year ago — maybe about a year and two months, if I’m trying to put a time on it.\n\nAt first I thought it was just a bad week, or that I was tired of the same routine. You know, sometimes you don’t feel like going out, and I told myself that was all it was. But then weeks kept passing, and the things that used to feel colorful, like watercolor class especially — which is funny, because it’s literally color — started feeling kind of flat. Like the color had drained out of it.\n\nSo yes, about fourteen months or so. It’s been hanging around that long, this low, colorless stretch.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you feel like doing those activities again, like watercolor class or quilting circle?\n\nPatient: Every now and then I think I do, or at least I think I should. Like I’ll tell myself, “You used to like watercolor, just go, you’ll feel better once you’re there.” And sometimes I even get the bag ready — the paints, the little pad, whatever I’m supposed to bring — and I’ll set it by the door so I can’t miss it.\n\nBut then when it’s time to leave, I just stand there looking at it. It’s not exactly that I decide not to go. It’s more like there’s no pull in me. The part that used to look forward to it just doesn’t seem to show up. Same with quilting circle, or book club. I used to have that feeling of, “Oh good, it’s Thursday, I get to go,” and now I mostly just don’t feel enough interest to get myself out the door.\n\nSo yes, I have days where I think about doing them, but actually wanting to go — really feeling that spark again — that doesn’t happen much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've gotten your interest back in those activities, even if it's just for a little while?\n\nPatient: Maybe for a flicker, but not really in the way I used to. Sometimes I’ll get this little idea in my head like, “Oh, maybe I’ll go to watercolor today,” and I’ll gather the paints and the paper and put the bag right by the door, because I’m trying to help myself along, you know? Like if it’s sitting there, then I’ll have no excuse.\n\nBut then the time comes and I just look at it. I know I used to look forward to that class, and to quilting circle, and book club too. I used to have that nice feeling of having somewhere to be, something I cared about. Now even when I try to push myself toward it, I can’t seem to make myself care enough to actually pick up the bag and go.\n\nSo I guess I might remember that I used to enjoy it, but feeling the interest come back… only briefly, if at all. It doesn’t last long enough to get me out the door most of the time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I look at it sitting there and I can’t seem to make myself care enough to pick it up and leave.\n\nIt’s not that I hate those people or that I decided I don’t like the activities anymore. I used to enjoy them. I just don’t feel that pull toward them like I used to, and then I feel bad afterward because I know I’ve missed something I normally would’ve cared about.\n\nDoctor: When did you start noticing you weren't enjoying your activities like watercolor class, quilting circle, and book club as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a long while now. I’d say I first really noticed it a little over a year ago — maybe about a year and two months, if I’m trying to put a time on it.\n\nAt first I thought it was just a bad week, or that I was tired of the same routine. You know, sometimes you don’t feel like going out, and I told myself that was all it was. But then weeks kept passing, and the things that used to feel colorful, like watercolor class especially — which is funny, because it’s literally color — started feeling kind of flat. Like the color had drained out of it.\n\nSo yes, about fourteen months or so. It’s been hanging around that long, this low, colorless stretch.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you feel like doing those activities again, like watercolor class or quilting circle?\n\nPatient: Every now and then I think I do, or at least I think I should. Like I’ll tell myself, “You used to like watercolor, just go, you’ll feel better once you’re there.” And sometimes I even get the bag ready — the paints, the little pad, whatever I’m supposed to bring — and I’ll set it by the door so I can’t miss it.\n\nBut then when it’s time to leave, I just stand there looking at it. It’s not exactly that I decide not to go. It’s more like there’s no pull in me. The part that used to look forward to it just doesn’t seem to show up. Same with quilting circle, or book club. I used to have that feeling of, “Oh good, it’s Thursday, I get to go,” and now I mostly just don’t feel enough interest to get myself out the door.\n\nSo yes, I have days where I think about doing them, but actually wanting to go — really feeling that spark again — that doesn’t happen much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've gotten your interest back in those activities, even if it's just for a little while?\n\nPatient: Maybe for a flicker, but not really in the way I used to. Sometimes I’ll get this little idea in my head like, “Oh, maybe I’ll go to watercolor today,” and I’ll gather the paints and the paper and put the bag right by the door, because I’m trying to help myself along, you know? Like if it’s sitting there, then I’ll have no excuse.\n\nBut then the time comes and I just look at it. I know I used to look forward to that class, and to quilting circle, and book club too. I used to have that nice feeling of having somewhere to be, something I cared about. Now even when I try to push myself toward it, I can’t seem to make myself care enough to actually pick up the bag and go.\n\nSo I guess I might remember that I used to enjoy it, but feeling the interest come back… only briefly, if at all. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really, no. I mean… I have moments where I remember what it felt like to be my old self, if that makes sense. Like I can remember looking forward to watercolor class, and thinking about what colors I might use, or being glad to see the ladies at quilting circle, or wanting to know what everyone thought at book club.

But actually feeling that way again? It’s rare. Most of the time I’ll get as far as setting things up — I’ll pack the watercolor bag or put the book somewhere I can see it, and I’ll think, “Alright, you’re going today.” And then when it’s time, I just sort of stop. I look at the bag by the door, and there’s nothing in me that reaches for it. It’s not that I hate going, exactly. It’s more like I can’t make myself care enough to go.

So I wouldn’t say I have real periods where I feel like my old self. Maybe a little flash of remembering her, but not really getting her back.

And then other days I do the opposite—I’ll stand at the counter and eat crackers or sweets, just picking at them, but not because I’m enjoying it. Nothing tastes quite right, so it doesn’t feel satisfying.\n\nSo maybe stress is part of it sometimes, but it doesn’t feel only like nerves. It feels like my appetite and interest in food are just turned around from how they used to be.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant weight loss recently?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about “significant” weight loss. I haven’t been keeping close track of the number, to be honest. What I noticed more is that my eating has just gotten all turned around, and that’s really what made me come in.\n\nSome days I’ll forget lunch completely—not on purpose, just the day goes by and I realize later I never ate. Then other days I do the opposite and I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, sort of picking at them, even though I’m not enjoying it and nothing tastes right. So whether my weight has changed a lot, I don’t know for sure, but my appetite and the way I’m eating definitely feel different.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything else going on that's making food less enjoyable for you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there’s one other thing I can point to, like one clear reason. It’s more that the whole pattern has changed, and that’s what bothers me.\n\nI’ve said it a bit already, but some days lunch just disappears from my mind completely. I’ll look up later and realize I never ate, not because I was trying to skip it, just because it didn’t occur to me. And then on other days, it’s almost the opposite — I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers, or something sweet, just sort of nibbling and picking at it. But I’m not really enjoying it. It’s not like, “Oh, this tastes good.” It’s just… there.\n\nAnd even foods I used to like don’t taste quite right to me now. So I don’t know if something else is making it less enjoyable, exactly. It feels more like my appetite and the pleasure in eating have gotten all turned around.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you enjoy activities other than eating less often now than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think so. It’s not just the food. I used to look forward to things, you know? I had my watercolor class, and my quilting circle, and book club. Those were the things that gave shape to the week.\n\nNow I’ll get the bag ready sometimes — like the watercolor things or whatever I’m supposed to bring — and I’ll leave it by the door, and then when it’s time to go I just… don’t. I look at it sitting there and I can’t seem to make myself care enough to pick it up and leave.\n\nIt’s not that I hate those people or that I decided I don’t like the activities anymore. I used to enjoy them. I just don’t feel that pull toward them like I used to, and then I feel bad afterward because I know I’ve missed something I normally would’ve cared about.\n\nDoctor: When did you start noticing you weren't enjoying your activities like watercolor class, quilting circle, and book club as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a long while now. I’d say I first really noticed it a little over a year ago — maybe about a year and two months, if I’m trying to put a time on it.\n\nAt first I thought it was just a bad week, or that I was tired of the same routine. You know, sometimes you don’t feel like going out, and I told myself that was all it was. But then weeks kept passing, and the things that used to feel colorful, like watercolor class especially — which is funny, because it’s literally color — started feeling kind of flat. Like the color had drained out of it.\n\nSo yes, about fourteen months or so. It’s been hanging around that long, this low, colorless stretch.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you feel like doing those activities again, like watercolor class or quilting circle?\n\nPatient: Every now and then I think I do, or at least I think I should. Like I’ll tell myself, “You used to like watercolor, just go, you’ll feel better once you’re there.” And sometimes I even get the bag ready — the paints, the little pad, whatever I’m supposed to bring — and I’ll set it by the door so I can’t miss it.\n\nBut then when it’s time to leave, I just stand there looking at it. It’s not exactly that I decide not to go. It’s more like there’s no pull in me. The part that used to look forward to it just doesn’t seem to show up. Same with quilting circle, or book club. I used to have that feeling of, “Oh good, it’s Thursday, I get to go,” and now I mostly just don’t feel enough interest to get myself out the door.\n\nSo yes, I have days where I think about doing them, but actually wanting to go — really feeling that spark again — that doesn’t happen much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've gotten your interest back in those activities, even if it's just for a little while?\n\nPatient: Maybe for a flicker, but not really in the way I used to. Sometimes I’ll get this little idea in my head like, “Oh, maybe I’ll go to watercolor today,” and I’ll gather the paints and the paper and put the bag right by the door, because I’m trying to help myself along, you know? Like if it’s sitting there, then I’ll have no excuse.\n\nBut then the time comes and I just look at it. I know I used to look forward to that class, and to quilting circle, and book club too. I used to have that nice feeling of having somewhere to be, something I cared about. Now even when I try to push myself toward it, I can’t seem to make myself care enough to actually pick up the bag and go.\n\nSo I guess I might remember that I used to enjoy it, but feeling the interest come back… only briefly, if at all. It doesn’t last long enough to get me out the door most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have periods where you feel like your old self again, with interest in the things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I have moments where I remember what it felt like to be my old self, if that makes sense. It’s rare. Most of the time I’ll get as far as setting things up — I’ll pack the watercolor bag or put the book somewhere I can see it, and I’ll think, “Alright, you’re going today.” And then when it’s time, I just sort of stop. I look at the bag by the door, and there’s nothing in me that reaches for it. It’s not that I hate going, exactly. It’s more like I can’t make myself care enough to go.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say I have real periods where I feel like my old self. Maybe a little flash of remembering her, but not really getting her back.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I used to have that feeling of, “Oh good, it’s Thursday, I get to go,” and now I mostly just don’t feel enough interest to get myself out the door.\n\nSo yes, I have days where I think about doing them, but actually wanting to go — really feeling that spark again — that doesn’t happen much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've gotten your interest back in those activities, even if it's just for a little while?\n\nPatient: Maybe for a flicker, but not really in the way I used to. Sometimes I’ll get this little idea in my head like, “Oh, maybe I’ll go to watercolor today,” and I’ll gather the paints and the paper and put the bag right by the door, because I’m trying to help myself along, you know? Like if it’s sitting there, then I’ll have no excuse.\n\nBut then the time comes and I just look at it. I know I used to look forward to that class, and to quilting circle, and book club too. I used to have that nice feeling of having somewhere to be, something I cared about. Now even when I try to push myself toward it, I can’t seem to make myself care enough to actually pick up the bag and go.\n\nSo I guess I might remember that I used to enjoy it, but feeling the interest come back… only briefly, if at all. It doesn’t last long enough to get me out the door most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have periods where you feel like your old self again, with interest in the things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… I have moments where I remember what it felt like to be my old self, if that makes sense. Like I can remember looking forward to watercolor class, and thinking about what colors I might use, or being glad to see the ladies at quilting circle, or wanting to know what everyone thought at book club.\n\nBut actually feeling that way again? It’s rare. Most of the time I’ll get as far as setting things up — I’ll pack the watercolor bag or put the book somewhere I can see it, and I’ll think, “Alright, you’re going today.” And then when it’s time, I just sort of stop. I look at the bag by the door, and there’s nothing in me that reaches for it. It’s not that I hate going, exactly. It’s more like I can’t make myself care enough to go.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say I have real periods where I feel like my old self. Maybe a little flash of remembering her, but not really getting her back.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had a period of two weeks or more where you felt consistently interested in your activities again?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No… not two weeks, no. I don’t think I’ve had anything that steady like that.

I’ve had maybe a day here or there where I think, “Maybe I should go back to watercolor,” or I’ll see the quilting bag and feel a little nudge, like maybe I ought to try. But it doesn’t hold. It’s not like I wake up for a couple weeks and feel interested again the way I used to.

Most of the time it’s the same pattern — I’ll set the bag by the door, or put the book out where I can see it, and I tell myself I’m going to go. But then when it comes time, I just can’t make myself care enough to actually leave. So no, I wouldn’t say I’ve had a two-week stretch of feeling consistently interested again.
